The genuinely actionable content lives at intake-946#record; see its dive_corrections for the transferable path, which runs through unsloth's already-published GGUFs of the same base model rather than through anything Escha ships.

### Verdict

worth_investigating. NOT not_applicable despite the runtime being unusable on our hardware - the target model makes it in-domain by construction. NOT adopt_component: hard technical foreclosure, the runtime is NVIDIA-only on proprietary CUDA kernels with no GGUF, no llama.cpp, no ROCm and no CPU path, and our GPU is gfx90a. NOT adopt_patterns yet: they publish NO methodology, so there is no pattern to extract, only an outcome to verify. NOT superseded: our own sub-2-bit line is quality-blocked, which is precisely why their claim is interesting.

Both HuggingFace repos are days old (first release around 2026-07-30) - a single-release org with no track record. Re-check in 60-90 days: a second release establishes a lineage worth investment; silence closes it.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add a monitor line to tq3-quantization-evaluation.md with ONE trigger: a GGUF export path or llama.cpp/ggml kernel for the W2 format. Absent that, no EPYC work is possible regardless of quality.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-B, scoped by the operator to a worker_general / architect_critic candidate assessment. Core filing SOUND and confirmed; the methodology-discipline note is UPHELD; one partial overturn and several material additions. (1) PARTIAL OVERTURN ON THE CODING GAP. The -4.4 point LiveCodeBench delta should NOT be recorded as an established magnitude. The card applies an ASYMMETRIC noise standard: it calls GPQA +3.1 at n=198 an "effective tie" citing ~+-5pp run-to-run instability, while calling LCB -4.4 at the SMALLER n=182 "the one clear 2-bit gap". A -4.4pp delta on n=182 is 8 questions against an unpaired binomial SE of ~5.0pp, i.e. ~0.9 sigma, and no CIs appear anywhere on the card. Record as DIRECTION-CORROBORATED, MAGNITUDE-UNRESOLVED. Note the asymmetry is ANTI-self-serving - it makes their product look worse - so it supports the card's honesty rather than impeaching it. (2) THE TRUNCATION CONFOUND IS CONFIRMED AND DOES NOT INVALIDATE RETENTION. Arithmetic is self-consistent (0.139 x 49.5 + 0.861 x 86.0 = 80.93 ~ 80.9) and near-symmetric across arms (13.9% vs FP8 13.0%), so it biases BOTH arms downward. Back-solving the completed-answer rates narrows the gap to ~1.2pp, marginally NARROWER than the 1.4pp headline. (3) THE THINKING-MODE SPLIT MATERIALLY WEAKENS APPLICABILITY TO OUR INTENDED USE. All six headline axes are thinking-ON. The only thinking-OFF evidence is Commonsense-6 (boolq/piqa/arc-e/arc-c/hellaswag/winogrande, 76.06 vs 75.10) - six saturated multiple-choice tasks. worker_general would run enable_thinking=false, so for that role the evidence base collapses to those six. (4) "int8 dense is lossless" rests on a SINGLE boolq comparison (88.38 vs 88.04), and 88.38 is the SAME CELL as the Commonsense-6 boolq entry - one measurement doing double duty. Restate as "indistinguishable on a single saturated benchmark, delta 0.34pp". (5) THE CONTEXT CONFLICT WITH intake-387#record IS RESOLVED AND IS NOT A LIMITATION. CTXLEN 32768 is a serving-recipe DEFAULT ("Raise once the defaults work"), and RULER is evaluated across 8k/32k/64k/128k. The card never claims to retain the base model's native window. File as "serving-recipe default, evaluated to 128k, 262K untested". (6) ZERO THIRD-PARTY EVALUATION as of this date. Five HF discussion threads are all community REQUESTS - including one explicitly asking for "Additional Benchmarks & KL Divergence vs. Base Model", i.e. an open request for exactly the corroboration that does not exist. The org's social post RESTATES its own numbers and inflates to "12 benchmarks" by counting Commonsense-6 sub-tasks; that is restatement, not corroboration. (7) Serving levers (CUDA graphs 4.4x, radix-cache disable ~20%) are confirmed as stated but are CUDA/SGLang artifacts with no llama.cpp analogue - NOT transferable. (8) THE ACTIONABLE PATH STAGE 1 MISSED, and the reason this entry matters at all. The artifact is unreachable (eschamoe is CUDA sm_80-120 only, no GGUF, no ROCm, no CPU) and that filing is correct - but the compression TARGET, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, is our production frontdoor model, is ~3B active against gemma4's ~4B, and is ALREADY supported by the frozen kernel (LLM_ARCH_QWEN35MOE with a dedicated llama_model_qwen35moe class and an mtp_on_hybrid_qwen35 path). unsloth already publishes UD-IQ2_XXS at 10.8 GB and UD-IQ3_XXS at 13.2 GB - BOTH UNDER worker_general's existing ram_gb 16 budget. So Escha's retention evidence is a reason to test OUR OWN low-bit GGUF of a model we already serve. Three constraints on that test: (a) THE SIZE-MATCHED QUANT IS THE WRONG QUANT - iqk_dispatch.cpp:73-74 in the frozen tree carries static_assert(!iqk_typeA_supported(GGML_TYPE_Q2_K)) and the same for Q3_K, making them the ONLY two K-quant types excluded from iqk, so UD-Q2_K_XL (12.3 GB, byte-for-byte Escha's footprint) would put unaccelerated types in the routed-expert bulk; UD-IQ3_XXS is the correct arm. (b) unsloth's low-bit files derive from STOCK and lack the 20 nextn/MTP tensors our production GGUF carries (753 vs 733), so draft-mtp would fall out of the composed recipe unless we requantize our own MTP GGUF with the already-built llama-quantize and llama-imatrix. (c) THE BAR IS HIGH: gemma4 runs 126.2/96.7/82.9 t/s fully optimized while 35B-A3B at Q8 on the same composed recipe reaches 69.89 t/s, so the low-bit build needs ~1.8x bandwidth-to-speed conversion just to tie - and tq3 already holds a counter-example where fewer bits ran 2.2x SLOWER because dequant did not vectorize. (9) ROLE VERDICT. worker_general: CONDITIONAL - no to Escha's artifact, yes to the experiment it motivates. architect_critic: NO, and closed by OUR OWN decision-grade evidence rather than Escha's - Qwen3.5-122B at UD-IQ2_M reached FR 58.3% on the C-CRAB reviewer slice against the incumbent UD-Q4_K_M's FA 45.8% / FR 41.7%, and Qwen3.6-27B dense Q8_0 with ZERO quantization damage over-approved at FA 54.2% / AUC 0.503. Low-bit does not preserve critic CALIBRATION, and Escha measured no calibration metric of any kind. (10) CORROBORATION THAT IS OURS, NOT THEIRS: intake-861#record / arXiv 2505.02390 already records that 2-bit erodes REASONING while KNOWLEDGE holds ~99%, and Escha's six-axis ordering independently matches that shape (knowledge 98.3, commonsense ~101, long-context 100.5, tool-use 100.8, CODE 93.4). It does not establish that a 2-bit 35B-A3B is good; it establishes where to look first.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns - take the method, refuse the artifact, the same shape the index already applied at intake-871#record. The artifact is unusable AND unwanted on two independent grounds. (1) No path to our stack: eschamoe has no GGUF representation and no ggml producer or transcode path, the only runtime is a CUDA sm_80-120 wheel, and our GPU is an MI210 gfx90a with no CPU path at all. Even a hypothetical eschamoe-to-ggml dequantizer would land at a higher-bit GGUF we can already produce directly from the FP8/BF16 base. (2) The problem it solves is not ours: the entire artifact exists to fit 35B-A3B into 16-24 GB of consumer VRAM, and we have 1.1 TB of DDR5 plus a 64 GB MI210 that already holds this exact model at Q8_0 - which is precisely the standing rationale in tq3-quantization-evaluation.md that we do not need VRAM savings. NOT not_applicable, because three things transfer and one is directly actionable on an open handoff.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/architect-model-selection-bench.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tq3-quantization-evaluation.md`

Our own bench already hit 15/16 reasoning-channel cap truncations under --enable-thinking; this card shows both the fix (a thinking-budget cap forcing the end-of-think token, giving zero non-terminations) and the magnitude of the bias (49.5% truncated versus 86.0% completed).

Our sub-2-bit probes died on instruction-format failures before reaching this test - this is a concrete reopen criterion for the tq3 stop-list.

Check against our own GGUF conversion.

## intake-947#record — HipKittens: Fast and Furious AMD Kernels

- **URL**: https://github.com/HazyResearch/HipKittens
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, local_inference)
- **Expanded from**: intake-944#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Tile-based DSL primitives generalize off NVIDIA to AMD: explicit tile-based programming with optimized memory accesses and fine-grained asynchronous execution.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-I. verdict adopt_patterns and credibility_score 5 both STAND, but the verdict now rests on ECONOMICS RATHER THAN CAPABILITY, which is a different argument.
1. Outperforms all available kernel baselines by 1.2-2.4x on d=64 attention, GQA backwards and memory-bound kernels.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-I. verdict adopt_patterns and credibility_score 5 both STAND, but the verdict now rests on ECONOMICS RATHER THAN CAPABILITY, which is a different argument.
2. Competes with AMD hand-optimized assembly kernels for GEMMs and attention, and consistently outperforms compiler baselines.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-I. verdict adopt_patterns and credibility_score 5 both STAND, but the verdict now rests on ECONOMICS RATHER THAN CAPABILITY, which is a different argument.
3. Support is scoped to CDNA3 and CDNA4 ONLY - stated verbatim in the README.
   - *narrowed*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-I. verdict adopt_patterns and credibility_score 5 both STAND, but the verdict now rests on ECONOMICS RATHER THAN CAPABILITY, which is a different argument. The README scoping to CDNA3/CDNA4 is verbatim and stands, but THE STATED FORECLOSURE MECHANISM IS WRONG and that changes what the scoping means for us. The entry claimed gfx90a 'lacks the CDNA3 asynchronous buffer-load and scheduling machinery the primitives are built around'. There is no such machinery: global_load_lds has ZERO occurrences in the entire HipKittens repo, and the CDNA3 load path is ordinary global_load_dwordx4. So gfx90a is EXCLUDED BY PROJECT SCOPE, not foreclosed by missing hardware capability — a materially different conclusion for anyone costing a port.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-I. verdict adopt_patterns STANDS and credibility_score 5 STANDS - but two material claims in this entry are OVERTURNED, and the verdict now rests on ECONOMICS rather than CAPABILITY. (1) THE STATED FORECLOSURE MECHANISM IS WRONG. This entry says gfx90a "lacks the CDNA3 asynchronous buffer-load and scheduling machinery the primitives are built around". THERE IS NO SUCH MACHINERY: global_load_lds has ZERO occurrences in the entire HipKittens repo. The CDNA3 load path is ordinary global_load_dwordx4 into VGPRs with s_waitcnt DEFERRED, then ds_write_b64 into LDS - manual software pipelining, every instruction of which exists on gfx90a. The paper's own D.5 claim that direct global-to-LDS is a CDNA3/CDNA4 feature is contradicted by LLVM, where FeatureVMemToLDSLoad is a member of FeatureGFX9 itself, and by our own repo, which already prescribes llvm.amdgcn.raw.buffer.load.lds on gfx90a at 4 B/lane - a NARROW direct-to-LDS DMA rather than a CDNA3-class async bulk copy, which reconciles the apparent contradiction with K28's categorical "NO async-copy/TMA/cp.async" (both are true at different granularities). Their own E.1 admits they do not use it on CDNA3 anyway. (2) THE STRUCTURAL CLAIMS ARE FALSE AS WRITTEN. KITTENS_CDNA2 EXISTS (include/cdna3/common/util.cuh:518, and on the cdna3 branch at common/util.cuh:126) plus THIRTEEN build files carrying -DKITTENS_CDNA2 --offload-arch=gfx90a; gfx90a appears in 20 files and CDNA2 in 14. The OPERATIVE fact survives - kittens.cuh has no CDNA2 dispatch arm so the flag includes no headers, and kernels/common.mk hard-errors on anything but CDNA3/CDNA4/UDNA1 - so restate as "A DANGLING BUILD ARM, NOT SUPPORT". MI210: 0 hits, confirmed. Telling detail: MAX_SHARED_MEMORY is IDENTICAL for CDNA2 and CDNA3 (65536), so gfx90a sits in the same 64 KB LDS regime the design already has a working variant for. (3) THE EXCLUSION DECOMPOSES CLEANLY. NOT fundamental: tile types, LDS layout, coalesced loads, wave64 shuffles, buffer-resource addressing, the 64 KB LDS budget, and the bf16/fp16 MFMA core - the two builtins HK's CDNA3 path uses are mfma_f32_16x16x16f16 (LLVM feature mai-insts) and mfma_f32_16x16x16bf16_1k (feature gfx90a-insts, literally named for our chip). GENUINELY fundamental: fp8/fp6/fp4 and MX-scaled MFMA (fp8-insts / gfx950-insts) plus the gfx950 wide-K shapes - and 100% of the project's current momentum lives there. MOOT: chiplet swizzling, worth 7-19% on MI355X but a non-issue on a single-GCD MI210. AND WE ALREADY HAVE THE bf16 LAYER: frozen v8 ships ggml/src/ggml-cuda/mma.cuh calling those exact two builtins (:1051, :1250, :1286) plus int8 MFMA, with explicit CDNA1/2/3/4 branches. The gap to HK on bf16 is TUNING QUALITY, not capability. (4) THE DECISIVE COUNTER-EVIDENCE AGAINST PORTING is in the paper. Figure 14, on MI325X (CDNA3, ONE GENERATION BACK), is the only chart with two arms and HK LOSES AT 3 OF 5 GEMM SIZES (N=4096 PyTorch 755 vs HK 752; N=8192 791 vs 755; N=16384 749 vs 716), paired with E.1's admission that at 65 KB shared memory "we cannot double buffer in shared memory". The design is already degrading at CDNA3; MI210 is a further generation back and entirely untested. A port is buildable and would probably not be worth building. (5) REPLACE THE reported_results NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE ROW WITH THE REAL TABLES. The 1.2-2.4x range is carried by GQA-backward d=128 (seq 8192 causal: SDPA 224 / CK 489 / AITER 272 / HK 933, i.e. 3.4x AITER) and d=64 attention (16384: AITER 633 vs HK 941), where AMD's assembly coverage is ABSENT. Where AMD actually hand-tuned, AITER STILL WINS: MHA backward d=128 causal 4 of 5, MHA forward d=128 causal 4 of 5, and hipBLASLt wins BF16 GEMM at 4096 and 16384. A citation that omits this would be misleading; the paper itself is candid about it. (6) THE AITER MERGE IS VERIFIED FIRST-PARTY, so the "+1 independent corroboration ... reportedly merging" is now EARNED rather than provisional: ROCm/aiter PR #2039 "Introduce HipKittens based nhead=128 MLA Kernel" MERGED 2026-03-09, 23 files, +3483/-480, with 11 HK kernel files live on main and AMD copyright headers. BUT IT DOES NOT REACH gfx90a, and the negative is stronger than expected: AITER's own Supported Hardware table lists gfx942 fully supported, gfx950 supported, and three RDNA CONSUMER parts as experimental - NO MI210, NO MI250, NO gfx90a AT ANY TIER. Consumer RDNA is supported ahead of our datacenter card. AMD's centralised kernel library has DROPPED CDNA2; this is the single most consequential procurement fact in the dive. (7) CORRECT the notes: "No .github directory appeared ... so automated CI is unconfirmed" -> CI IS CONFIRMED ABSENT (.github 404s on main). Also note four AMD employees among the authors, and zero mentions of gfx90a, MI210, MI250 or CDNA2 anywhere in the 41-page paper. (8) FAIRNESS CAVEATS to carry with any citation: hipBLASLt is run WITHOUT any tuning or algo-search flag while the NVIDIA baseline is explicitly autotuned - an asymmetry that mildly flatters HK against AMD baselines; CDNA3 numbers come from a different container than CDNA4 numbers so the MI325X panel is not stack-comparable; correctness is one sentence with no table; and there is no Limitations or Future Work section. (9) TRANSFERABLE SCIENCE, arch-independent and free: DO NOT WAVE-SPECIALIZE (Tab. 2 - 0 producers/8 consumers = 1610 TFLOPs against 4 producers/8 consumers = 893, because AMD hardware statically divides registers across all waves so producers consume registers without contributing); 8-wave ping-pong before 4-wave interleave (Tab. 3 - 48 LoC/3222 TFLOPs vs 183 LoC/3327, 4x less code for ~90% of performance); LDS bank/phase behaviour is UNDOCUMENTED in the CDNA ISA and must be solved empirically (their Tab. 5 answer does not transfer, THE METHOD DOES); swizzle on the HBM side, not the LDS side; and HIPCC will not feed AGPRs to matrix-core instructions, forcing redundant v_accvgpr_read - a live compiler tax on gfx90a and exactly what a profiler-metric analyzer should learn to recognise. STAGE-2b ADDENDUM 2026-08-03 (dive 2b-B, HipKittens harvest) - four mechanisms extracted, one honest negative, and the composition result that makes them free. THE COMPOSITION RESULT: HK's register tile is BIT-IDENTICAL to the one already in our frozen v8. HK rt_base row layout is row = laneid%16, col = 4*(laneid/16), 4 elements per lane; ggml-cuda/mma.cuh:127,144 under AMD_MFMA_AVAILABLE gives get_i = tid%16, get_j = 4*(tid/16)+l, ne=4. SAME OBJECT. Every HK technique composes onto existing ggml fragments with ZERO layout re-derivation. A LIVE gfx90a BUILD ARM EXISTS on the `cdna3` branch: GPU_TARGET=CDNA2 -> -DKITTENS_CDNA2 --offload-arch=gfx90a (tests/unit/Makefile:39-40). Full inventory of all 67 headers: the library uses exactly SIX __builtin_amdgcn_* intrinsics and EXACTLY ONE is unavailable on gfx90a (mfma_f32_16x16x32_fp8_fp8, mma.cuh:47) - non-template static inline, so it needs an #if guard. `main` errors on CDNA2; `cdna3` already has the arm wired, carrying a ~3000-test correctness harness that would run on our silicon. THIS IS WHY THE DECLINE TO PORT IS AN ECONOMICS DECISION, NOT A CAPABILITY ONE. TRANSPOSE IS FREE ON CDNA: mma_AB/ABt/AtB/AtBt all bottom out in the same mfma161616 - A-row and B-col fragments are register-identical. NVIDIA needs ldmatrix.trans; AMD gets it free. SWIZZLE, MADE CONCRETE: addr ^ (((addr % swizzle_repeat) >> 7) << 3), with swizzle_repeat = swizzle_bytes << 4 = 2048 for bf16. Conflict-freedom verified numerically: 16 lanes -> 16 distinct starting banks, zero conflicts. THE DEEP COUPLING is that the per-row-block stride is ALWAYS an exact multiple of swizzle_repeat, which is why the LDS address can be hoisted out of the height loop and the rest encoded as compile-time `offset:` immediates - H address computations collapse to 1. DIRECT-TO-LDS, REFINED: CDNA4 DOES use llvm_amdgcn_raw_buffer_load_lds. The earlier "zero occurrences" was true only of __builtin_amdgcn_global_load_lds; the MUBUF form is used. AND WE ALREADY CALL THE SAME INTRINSIC ON gfx90a (mmvq.cu:538,602) - but we deposit LINEARLY. HK inverts the swizzle into the GLOBAL offset because a fixed-mapping DMA cannot XOR the LDS address. That is "swizzle HBM-side" made concrete, and it is what we add when the LDS consumer becomes a 2-D MFMA tile read. SCHEDULING IS NOT IN THE LIBRARY - zero occurrences of setprio / sched_barrier / s_barrier across all 67 headers. Every schedule is open-coded per kernel. THAT IS EXACTLY THE ABSTRACTION GAP AN AUTOKERNEL STACK FILLS. The 8-wave ping-pong mechanism: offset-barrier team split (team 1 takes an EXTRA s_barrier so it runs permanently one cluster behind), s_setprio(1)/(0) bracketing MFMA blocks, sched_barrier(0) after every cluster, and a mirror-image parity restore before the epilogue OR THE WORKGROUP HANGS. THE LDS BANK/PHASE SOLVER IS A REAL RUNNABLE ARTIFACT: analysis/paper_experiments/phases/*/ {bank_solver.py, phase_solver.py, kernel.cpp} - a 45-line kernel over rocprofv3 PMC counters, ~40 min GPU on gfx90a. CDNA3/4 answer is 64 banks, 2 phases of 32 lanes. DO NOT ASSUME IT TRANSFERS - whether gfx90a is 32 or 64 banks decides whether the >>7 <<3 swizzle constants transfer at all. GRID SWIZZLE MUST BE SWEPT, NOT SET (measured): WGM none 1016.6 TFLOPs / 2 -> +6.6% / 4 -> +9.1% / 8 -> +9.6% / 32 -> -13.9%. Pure launch-order reordering, zero kernel-body change. The chiplet half does NOT apply (MI210 = 1 GCD); the L2-locality half does. HONEST NEGATIVE, recorded so it is not re-derived: FOR A DEQUANT GAP, HK HAS NOTHING. Its quantized path is gfx950 HARDWARE block-scaling (mfma_scale_f32_*_f8f6f4); there is no software dequant anywhere. Its cross-lane reductions are WORSE than what we would write (a plain __shfl_down ladder, no DPP/permlane) - a place we can BEAT it. THE AMD/NVIDIA GAP AS CODE: (a) no async DMA engine - TK's TMA/cp.async/mbarrier becomes hand-rolled VGPR staging with manual s_waitcnt; (b) no SMEM-operand tensor core - WGMMA's st_st form has no MFMA counterpart, so EVERY operand goes through the register file (hence HK's own "TODO: hack to avoid too many VGPRs"); (c) no setmaxnreg, so warp specialization is both unavailable AND unwanted; (d) no cluster/DSMEM tier; (e) no ldmatrix.trans before gfx950. LICENCE MIT. DO NOT VENDOR THE FRAMEWORK (C++20 concepts-heavy; ggml already has the identical tile). Lift the four mechanisms as self-contained additions with provenance headers.

### Verdict

Technically excellent and hardware-foreclosed for us, but the foreclosure rests on ECONOMICS, not capability — this entry's original claim that gfx90a lacks the required async machinery is OVERTURNED. Do not repeat the 'CDNA2 lacks the machinery' mechanism; cite the gfx942/gfx950 gating and the porting cost instead.
adopt_patterns, following the house precedent at intake-871#record and intake-756#record for work that is technically excellent but hardware-foreclosed. Not adopt_component: support is gated to gfx942/gfx950 in both prose and the arch-dispatch header, with no CDNA2 fallback path, and gfx90a lacks the CDNA3 asynchronous buffer-load and scheduling machinery the primitives are built around - this is a port project we have no mandate for. Not not_applicable: the scheduling and layout ideas are arch-independent and directly useful to anyone hand-writing MI210 MFMA kernels. Not superseded: nothing displaces it.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/agentic-rocm-kernel-authoring.md`
- updated `handoffs/completed/mi210-mfma-compute-bound-paths.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/mi210-q8-dequant-gemv-roofline.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/rocm-verify-profile-backend.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/gpu-acceleration-path.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Record HipKittens in the hardware-acceleration index as CDNA3-ONLY, citing the include/kittens.cuh dispatch evidence, so no future session repeats the intake-679#record CDNA3-as-drop-in error.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Mine the ideas, not the code: the 8-wave ping-pong and 4-wave interleave scheduling patterns and the bank-conflict-free tile layouts are arch-independent design lessons for our hand-written gfx90a MFMA paths.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Treat the reported merge of HK kernels into AMD AITER as a procurement datapoint for mi210-big-model-and-acceleration-roadmap.md: the AMD kernel-quality frontier is now CDNA3+, which widens the MI210 gap.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-H. verdict adopt_component and novelty high both STAND. The corpus defect is WORSE than filed and the liftable asset is BIGGER. (1) THE CORPUS NEVER EXISTED - the "unresolved" dangling reference is RESOLVED. All four cited paths 404; the file is absent on ALL THREE branches; the repo has 0 tags, 0 releases and 8 commits on main. Commit 18c4c582, whose message claims "align defaults with bundled corpus", has a diff touching ONLY examples/quick-run.sh. No commit ever added the file. It is referenced from FIVE files across THREE different paths, and examples/quick-run.sh:43 sets it as the EXECUTABLE DEFAULT - so the shipped quickstart is broken out of the box. This is a checklist-omission defect of exactly the class our own W7 work names. (2) THE MATCHER IS THE ASSET, and it is strictly better than the dedup in intake-943#record. Location comparison is RANGE OVERLAP, not line-exact (matcher.go:326-334, with missing EndLine collapsing to StartLine). Path normalisation strips container mounts /target/, /src/, /app/ and falls back to unique-suffix matching where AMBIGUITY YIELDS NO MATCH. CWE comparison is a MITRE-HIERARCHY DISTANCE rather than string equality (cwe/cwe.go:83-127): 0 identical, 1 direct parent/child or a curated pair, 2 siblings or shared category, 3+ common ancestor, -1 unrelated - with PILLARS EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED as meeting points ("everything is under a pillar, so meeting there is noise") and a hand-curated mechanism-to-consequence list covering 915->269, 347->287, 620->352, 862->{863,639} and 328->327. Five tiers (exact 1.0 / hierarchy 0.95-0.75 / fuzzy 0.9-0.5 within 5 lines / category 0.5-0.3 within 20 lines with CWE-relatedness REQUIRED / same_line 0.2). Assignment is strict 1-to-1 greedy with sort.SliceStable and two deterministic tiebreaks - startGap then lower annotation ID - and the code comment records that sort.Slice's INSTABILITY made assignment sensitive to unrelated changes. DETERMINISTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, which is load-bearing for replay under our measurement constitution. CWE SETS are supported, taking the best distance over the whole set. Group propagation synthesises match_type='group' rows at 0.4 so one consolidated finding scores TP against every endpoint in a systemic gap. (3) FALSE-POSITIVE ATTRIBUTION, which is the axis intake-845#record says we lack: TP = matched a status:valid annotation; FP = matched a status:INVALID decoy OR matched nothing; FN = valid unmatched; TN = invalid unmatched; needs_review counts as FP (conservative default). Counting is VULNERABILITY-LEVEL, so a vuln with six evidence rows and one match scores ONE TP, not six. THE DUAL-GOLD MECHANISM IS THE status:"invalid" DECOYS - deliberately suspicious-looking non-vulnerabilities, without which precision is unmeasurable. (4) THE IMPORTABLE ENVELOPE (vulns.go:15-71) is our dual-gold label schema: {name, description, criticality: must|should|may, status: valid|invalid|disputed, cwes: [a SET, any one matches], annotated_by: [len() IS the consensus level], evidence: [{file, line, end, role, category, severity}]}. Import guardrails worth copying: duplicate names are rejected, and re-import without --replace is blocked on overlap because the second copy is "a permanent phantom FN" that silently halves recall. (5) DOWNGRADE ONE CLAIM: the "cross-iteration variance with a confidence-interval overlap test" is a +-1 sigma interval-INTERSECTION HEURISTIC (cmd/benchmrk/analyze.go:558-567) that flags the best result as tied. It is NOT a confidence interval and NOT a significance test. Do not inherit the framing into scoring-infra-standardization.md. (6) RESOLVE the SKILLS.md/AGENTS.md open question: there is NO LLM-scanner integration path. The path is the generic local-wrapper contract (SKILLS.md:166-227) - SARIF 2.1.0 (or semgrep-json) written to exactly $OUTPUT_DIR/results.sarif, a minimal env, and docker --network none. SINCE codecrucible EMITS SARIF, A THREE-LINE WRAPPER MAKES IT A FIRST-CLASS benchmrk SCANNER: the two repos compose without either knowing about the other. (7) ADD an undersold asset: examples/vulnerability-coverage/ai-annotation-prompt.md is a complete LLM-ASSISTED GROUND-TRUTH AUTHORING PROMPT - one-bug-many-evidence modelling, CWE-set population, criticality assignment, and an explicit instruction to author status:"invalid" decoys. It is the recipe for building the corpus benchmrk does not ship. (8) Coverage-overlap analysis (coverage.go) answers the multi-reviewer question directly: union recall vs best-single recall, per-scanner marginal contribution, blind spots caught by nobody, and flaky coverage - computed over VALID vulns only, because "an 'invalid' decoy caught by all scanners isn't coverage, it's a shared false positive".

### Verdict

adopt_component rather than adopt_patterns because it is a runnable Apache-2.0 Go binary with a tested matching engine we would otherwise have to write, and the annotation envelope is directly usable as our dual-gold label schema. The maturity caveat is real and recorded: 5 stars, 9 commits, one human committer, zero published results. It is adoptable as a scoring engine, not as evidence.

### Claims (as verified)

0. Wall-clock timing of short competitive-programming binaries is too noisy to rank candidate edits.

THE 1.91x IS A TAIL STATISTIC, not a typical result: v5 Sec 2 benchmarked 500 program PAIRS where the slow and fast programs are IDENTICAL and observed a MEAN speedup of 1.12x with SD 0.36, the 1.91x being the top 5%. Quoting 1.91x as the expected effect overstates it by roughly 70%.
1. Deterministic microarchitectural simulation replaces wall clock as the measurement instrument, giving fully deterministic results and reproducibility.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. Claims verified, two open items closed from the REPO rather than the paper, and one framing correction that changes how the entry may be used — carry it with any citation. THE 1.91x IS A TAIL STATISTIC, not a typical result: v5 Sec 2 benchmarked 500 program PAIRS where the slow and fast programs are IDENTICAL and observed a MEAN speedup of 1.12x with SD 0.36, the 1.91x being the top 5%. Quoting 1.91x as the expected effect overstates it by roughly 70%.

   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. THE 1.91x IS A TAIL STATISTIC, not a typical result: v5 Sec 2 benchmarked 500 program PAIRS where the slow and fast programs are IDENTICAL and observed a MEAN speedup of 1.12x with SD 0.36, the 1.91x being the top 5%. Quoting 1.91x as the expected effect overstates it by roughly 70%.

   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. Claims verified, two open items closed from the REPO rather than the paper, and one framing correction that changes how the entry may be used — carry it with any citation. THE 1.91x IS A TAIL STATISTIC, not a typical result: v5 Sec 2 benchmarked 500 program PAIRS where the slow and fast programs are IDENTICAL and observed a MEAN speedup of 1.12x with SD 0.36, the 1.91x being the top 5%. Quoting 1.91x as the expected effect overstates it by roughly 70%.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. Claims verified; two open items closed from the REPO rather than the paper; one framing correction that changes how the entry may be used. (1) THE 1.91x IS CONFIRMED VERBATIM AND IS A TAIL STATISTIC. v5 Sec 2: "We benchmarked 500 programs 'pairs' where the 'slow' and 'fast' programs are identical ... we observed a mean speedup of 1.12x, with a standard deviation of 0.36, and the top 5% of pairs exhibited a speedup of 1.91x." Byte-identical wording in v4. Its ABSENCE from v1 is confirmed by grep (zero hits for gem5, hyperfine, 1.91 or noise). (2) THE PROTOCOL IS RECOVERED FROM THE OFFICIAL REPO, closing an item Stage 1 marked NOT-FOUND. github.com/LearningOpt/pie: gem5/benchmarking.py:407 sets warmup_runs_per_test_case = 5; every call site passes min_runs=10, max_runs=500, warmup=5 and a cpu_number; :453,457 build `taskset --cpu-list N hyperfine --min-runs 10 --max-runs 500 --warmup 5 -N`; dual_submission benchmarks BOTH pair members in ONE hyperfine invocation ON THE SAME PINNED CORE; compilation is -O3 -std=c++17. THIS STRENGTHENS THE FINDING: the 1.91x tail survived warm-up, 10-500 repetitions and single-core pinning, so it is not a naive-harness artifact. Caveat: gem5/README.md:59 self-describes hyperfine support as "not fully implemented yet", so attribution to this exact harness is strong inference rather than proof. (3) STILL NOT-FOUND, and it matters: the HOST HARDWARE for the identical-pair experiment appears in neither the paper (11 appendices, none on benchmarking setup) nor the repo. The only hardware named is a 24-core i9-13900k in Appendix A.6, and THAT IS THE SELF-PLAY DATA-GENERATION HOST, NOT THE BENCHMARKING HOST - the entry must say so or someone will misattribute it. Also NOT-FOUND anywhere: any runtime-versus-noise relationship. CONSEQUENCE: THE 1.91x CANNOT BE SCALED TO MILLISECOND-SCALE GPU KERNELS, and our promotion threshold must come from our own A/A run rather than from this number. (4) THE MITIGATION IS CONFIRMED AS gem5 SIMULATION, NOT HARDWARE PERFORMANCE COUNTERS - zero mentions of performance counters anywhere. NEW CAVEAT: even the determinism is conditional, per footnote 2 - "This assumes gem5 terminates. Our experiments use a two-minute timeout, which may introduce slight variability. Note that altering this timeout could change the results." (5) THE 1.91x NOW HAS ZERO INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION IN THE COMPENDIUM. intake-954#record (COFFE) states the same figure but CITES THIS PAPER EXPLICITLY ("Shypula et al. [75] find that two single time measurements ... can differ as much as 1.91x"), so it is ONE SOURCE, NOT TWO - and the restatement is LOSSY, reframing a 95th-percentile tail over 500 pairs as a bound on what two measurements "can differ as much as". Always cite PIE Sec 2 directly, WITH the tail framing. This entry's credibility_score 3 is unaffected (its +1 came from intake-939#record, genuinely disjoint), but the FIGURE ITSELF must be demoted from "a measured noise floor we can cite" to "an unreproducible single-source anecdote that motivates our own A/A calibration". (6) VERSION DRIFT, not error: this entry declares v4 and its numbers are correct for v4, but v5 revised %OPT 87.68 -> 87.63, test pairs 982 -> 978, and COLLAPSED v4's two human references (Best Human 4.06x, Same Human 3.64x) into a single 3.66x, reframing the abstract from "surpassing the best human performance (4.06x)" to "higher than average optimizations from individual programmers (3.66x)" - a WEAKENING of the headline. The entry currently mixes v4 numbers with v5 framing. The noise numbers are identical across both versions. (7) Appendix A.9's 2.44x is CodeNet's own judge-reported CPUTime - a DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT. Do not merge it with the Hyperfine identical-pair run.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns. The modeling half is stale - 2023-era CODEX, gpt-3.5 and CodeLlama arms - and is not worth importing. The measurement half is directly load-bearing. Their mitigation (swap the instrument for a deterministic simulator) is unavailable to us on gfx90a, so the transferable artifact is the identical-pair null EXPERIMENT as a protocol, not their solution. Not adopt_component - gem5 is not portable to our target. Not not_applicable - it is squarely on the autokernel critical path.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/architect-model-selection-bench.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Run the identical-pair null experiment on our own harness BEFORE trusting any autokernel ranking: submit N>=500 A-versus-A identical kernel pairs, publish the mean, standard deviation and top-5% spurious speedup as the loop measured noise floor, and set the promotion threshold above the TAIL, not above 1.0.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Adopt the correctness-gated %OPT shape (a candidate must be BOTH correct and at least X% faster to count) as the autokernel promotion predicate, replacing any bare wall-clock argmax.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] gem5 is not portable to gfx90a. The analogue is a deterministic instrument - fixed clock, pinned, counter-based cycle counts, or replay. Scope a determinism study rather than assuming counters fix it, because THIS PAPER DOES NOT CLAIM THEY DO.

## intake-951#record — SWE-Perf: Can Language Models Optimize Code Performance on Real-World Repositories?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12415
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology)
- **Expanded from**: intake-939#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. First benchmark specifically designed to evaluate LLMs on code performance optimization within authentic repository contexts.
   - *unaffected*: The only framing correction is that the THIRD-AXIS FRAMING IS OVERTURNED (it is already correctness-gated); repository-context priority is never disputed.
   - anchor `matched span in https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12415`: "To address this gap, we introduce SWE-Perf, the first benchmark specifically designed to systematically evaluate LLMs on code performance optimization tasks within authentic repository contexts."
1. 140 curated instances, each derived from a performance-improving pull request, shipping codebase, target functions, performance tests, expert patches and executable environments.
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed while deleting a bogus defect: enumerating all 140 instances gives exactly nine repositories. Only the container pinning is weaker than described (nano_cpus, 10.0 CPUs).
2. A substantial capability gap exists between current LLMs and expert-level optimization performance.
   - *unaffected*: Survives the dive's own re-scoping: the per-method Performance_pass ceiling (8.3/8.8/11.4) leaves it robust to subset normalisation at 6.8% versus 25.4%.
   - anchor `matched span in https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12415`: "Through a comprehensive evaluation of representative methods that span file-level and repo-level approaches (e.g., Agentless and OpenHands), we reveal a substantial capability gap between existing LLMs and expert-level optimization performance, highlighting critical research opportunities in this..."
3. Models and experts optimize DIFFERENT things: agent patches focus on low-level data structures while expert patches emphasize high-level abstractions and data integrity.
   - *unaffected*: No correction touches the patch-content analysis; the dive's targets are licence, repo count, third-axis framing, pinning, cross-hardware delta, supply chain and author linkage.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-D. Three claims in this entry are overturned; verdict adopt_patterns STANDS but for a DIFFERENT REASON than filed. (1) THE LICENCE CLAIM IS BOTH STALE AND TOO GENEROUS. The restrictive "academic research license ... restricting deployment in sensitive or safety-critical systems" language is v1 Appendix A.2 ONLY and was DELETED ENTIRELY in the v2 ICML camera-ready, whose Impact Statement instead says the benchmark is released "publicly to facilitate reproducibility and community adoption". Stage 1 was right about v1 and stale on v2. THE OPERATIVE FACT IS WORSE THAN "UNRESOLVED": the CODE REPOSITORY HAS NO LICENCE FILE AT ALL - no LICENSE, COPYING or NOTICE on any branch, GitHub API "license": null - and issue #6, "Under what license is this code?", has been OPEN SINCE 2025-11-07 WITH ZERO MAINTAINER REPLIES IN NINE MONTHS. Default copyright is all rights reserved, which fails our open-source-only policy outright. The DATASET is separately Apache-2.0. (2) DELETE THE "ELEVEN REPOSITORY NAMES AGAINST A STATED COUNT OF NINE" ITEM - THE DISCREPANCY DOES NOT EXIST. The competing number is TWELVE, not eleven ("we adopt the same 12 repositories used in SWE-bench", in both v1 and v2), and Table 1 states 9. Enumerating all 140 instances gives exactly nine repositories: xarray 54, sklearn 32, sympy 20, astropy 12, sphinx 8, seaborn 6, matplotlib 3, pylint 3, requests 2 = 140. django, flask and pytest drop to zero. The crawl set is 12 and the benchmark is 9; there is no inconsistency. (3) THE THIRD-AXIS FRAMING IS OVERTURNED. SWE-Perf's Performance metric averages over N_total, ALL SAMPLES, so a failing patch contributes 0 - it is ALREADY CORRECTNESS-GATED at the instance level, exactly like SWE-fficiency, EffiBench-X and COFFE. It is not offering an uncoupled third axis; it is offering a fused score with the correctness column printed next to it, plus a genuinely separate Performance_pass view in Fig. 6 where the expert ceiling is RE-COMPUTED PER METHOD (8.3/8.8/11.4) - which is our own gate-scope-must-match-measured-subset axiom. REFRAME contradicting_evidence: the REPORTED COLUMNS rank differently (Agentless 88.57 Apply / 70.71 Correctness / 0.41% Performance against OpenHands 87.86 / 77.86 / 2.26%, robust to subset normalisation at 6.8% vs 25.4%), which argues for REPORTING correctness alongside - not against fusing. (4) THE RELEASED PINNING IS WEAKER THAN OUR OWN PROTOCOL and weaker than the paper describes. docker_build.py:541-544 sets nano_cpus only - a CFS BANDWIDTH QUOTA, NOT AFFINITY - with no cpuset_cpus, no cpuset_mems and no NUMA awareness anywhere in the harness; and the default resolves to 10.0 CPUs, not the 5 the paper and README state. The statistical protocol (3-test warm-up, 20 repetitions, IQR at k=1, Mann-Whitney U at alpha=0.1, conservative-minimum-gain delta with a delta > 0.05 practical floor) is CONFIRMED VERBATIM and remains the most valuable thing here. (5) CROSS-HARDWARE COMPARABILITY IS RESOLVED - NEGATIVE. delta is the largest gain surviving Mann-Whitney at alpha=0.1, so on a noisier host significance is lost earlier and delta is SYSTEMATICALLY DEFLATED: the metric is hardware-dependent through the noise channel and conservative-biased. The paper compounds this by pooling two machines with no stated instance-to-machine assignment and no cross-machine calibration. (6) SUPPLY-CHAIN RISK: run_evaluation.py:314 HARDCODES docker_image_prefix = 'docker.io/betty1202/' - a PERSONAL Docker Hub account, not a flag or env var - with 280 matching images at ~1.29 GB each, i.e. ~180 GB compressed for 140 instances, already over our 158 GB budget. (7) REPO VITALS: 11 commits, last 2025-10-28, 0 tags, 0 releases, 53 stars, 4 open issues. The scaffolds are NOT vendored - two stub READMEs pointing at Agentless and OpenHands, one link reported broken. (8) AUTHOR-LINKAGE CORRECTION: Mingzhe Du co-authors BOTH this entry and intake-952#record (EffiBench-X), and See-Kiong Ng / Luu Anh Tuan link EffiBench-X to Mercury. THE TWO ENTRIES ARE NOT INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS and must not be counted as mutual corroboration. intake-953#record (Harvard/Google) and intake-954#record (CUHK/ZJU) are genuinely independent of them and of each other. STAGE-2b ADDENDUM 2026-08-03 (dive 2b-D) - THE LINEAGE FINDING, and it changes how this cluster counts. Mingzhe Du is the hub on three papers: Mercury (intake-972#record) and EffiBench-X share 3 authors (Du, Ng, Luu Anh Tuan); Mercury and SWE-Perf share 2 (Du, Qian Liu); SWE-Perf and EffiBench-X share 1 (Du). EffiBench-X additionally inherits EffiBench (4 of 5 authors carry over). So Mercury -> EffiBench(-X) -> SWE-Perf IS ONE CONNECTED COMPONENT OF FOUR PAPERS, and Mercury is its ancestor and connector. CONSEQUENCE: the apparent field-wide consensus that "efficiency scores must be normalized against a reference distribution" is HALF ONE LINEAGE. The genuinely independent instruments DIVERGE sharply - ENAMEL (intake-970#record) normalizes against ONE EXPERT SOLUTION with censoring; EvalPerf (intake-971#record) against a CLUSTERED LADDER using instruction counts; GSO (intake-973#record) against a SINGLE HUMAN COMMIT in the same container; COFFE (intake-954#record) against THE BEST GROUND TRUTH, binary. COUNT THE DESIGN SPACE AS FOUR, NOT EIGHT. CLEAN NOTE: SWE-fficiency (intake-953#record, Harvard/GDM) is OUTSIDE the Du bloc and cited EvalPerf (UIUC), so the disputed normalization claim is a CROSS-LINEAGE MISREADING - a citation-hygiene failure, not a captured literature. THREE DISTINCT LIUS, DO NOT MERGE: Jiawei Liu (EvalPerf, UIUC), Qian Liu (Mercury/SWE-Perf, Sea AI Lab), Jinjian Liu (GSO, Berkeley). The "Liu et al., 2024" ambiguity is precisely how the original misattribution became plausible.

### Verdict

Adopt the statistical protocol (warm-up plus repetitions) — that is the valuable part and it ports regardless of licence. Do NOT repeat this entry's original licence characterisation: the restrictive language was v1-only and was deleted in the v2 camera-ready.
adopt_patterns rather than adopt_component precisely because the licence is unresolved - we cannot commit to the artifact under our sourcing policy. The statistical protocol ports regardless of licence, and it is the most valuable part: warm-up plus 20 repetitions plus IQR filtering plus a Mann-Whitney significance gate plus a conservative minimum gain is a ready-made noise discipline for our own timing harness. Not not_applicable and not superseded - it is complementary to intake-952#record/953/954 rather than displaced by them.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/architect-model-selection-bench.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Port the noise protocol, not the tasks: adopt warm-up plus 20 repetitions plus IQR at k=1 plus Mann-Whitney p<0.1 plus conservative-minimum-gain as the gate before any efficiency score enters our coding ladder.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add Performance as a THIRD axis alongside Apply and Correctness rather than folding it into a single score - their own data shows the three axes rank models differently. Resolve against the opposing COFFE design (intake-954#record) before implementing either.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Self-host feasibility: 140 Docker instances at 5 pinned cores fits our box trivially and could run many instances in parallel, but co-residency would violate the timing assumptions - this needs a region claim under the benchmarking protocol.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-D. verdict adopt_component HOLDS AND STRENGTHENS; both UNRESOLVED items are closed; three operational risks added. (1) THE DECISIVE QUESTION RESOLVES IN OUR FAVOUR, UNPATCHED. effibench/llm.py calls load_dotenv() and then constructs OpenAI() WITH NO ARGUMENTS via get_lm_client(), so the SDK's environment path applies: setting OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://localhost:PORT/v1 plus a dummy OPENAI_API_KEY retargets it at llama-server with no code change. The model name is free-form with no allowlist, and there are no litellm/openrouter/anthropic/vertex imports and no hardcoded endpoint anywhere. Caveat: reasoning-tag stripping is NAME-BASED, so name the served model accordingly or disable thinking server-side. (2) LICENCE CONFIRMED FROM THE LICENSE FILE rather than the README: 201 lines, one line from canonical upstream Apache-2.0. Caveats worth recording: the copyright holder was never filled in (line 189 still reads "Copyright [yyyy] [name of copyright owner]"), there is no NOTICE, setup.py declares no license=, and the licence was added five months after the initial commit. Vendored third_party/llm-sandbox is MIT. (3) RESOLVE "per-language problem-count breakdown unresolved": 623 problems IN EVERY LANGUAGE, uniformly - 3,738 total. Confirmed three independent ways: the paper's Table 14 denominators are x/623 per language and y/3738 overall; Table 1 states 623; and parquet null-counts read via HTTP range requests give nonnull=623 / nulls=0 for all six language columns. Generation is 3,738 prompts per model. (4) RESOLVE "single-execution vs averaged": SINGLE EXECUTION, established two ways. The code contains no repeat, n_runs, median, best_of or warmup anywhere in evaluate_solution.py, run_tests.py or backend_utils.py, and session.run() is called once with no repetition flag. Independently, Table 2's values are BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE MINIMUM of Table 6's three-run study for both audited models across ET, MP and MI - 6 of 6. The direction is CONSERVATIVE (it understates efficiency), so this is a rigor gap rather than a flattering-number problem. (5) CORRECT THE TECHNIQUE LIST: -O2 -fsanitize=address is documented in Table 13 and the flags key exists in EFFIBENCH_REGISTRY, BUT THE flags KEY IS NEVER READ - the actual command in llm_sandbox/utils.py is g++ -std=c++20 -O2 -o a.out. The paper and the shipped harness disagree, so THE PUBLISHED C++ TABLE IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE WITH THE RELEASED CODE. (6) HARDEN THE CONTAMINATION NOTE - the filter is inadequate for our use and unverifiable for half the benchmark. Reading release_timestamp for all 623 rows: ZERO rows pre-October-2023, range 2023-11-05 to 2024-12-22, i.e. ENTIRELY INSIDE every 2026-era model's training window. Worse, 315 of 623 (50.6%) CARRY NO DATE AT ALL - every AtCoder, CodeChef, Aizu and Codeforces problem, all 285 I/O problems, plus 30 LeetCode. Only the 308 dated LeetCode/functional rows are checkable, and the paper reports I/O scores 15-30pp lower. (7) THREE OPERATIONAL RISKS, one of them a defect class we have named ourselves. (a) FAIL-OPEN METRIC CORRUPTION, the highest-priority risk: open issue #4 (2026-03-09, unanswered) reports "runtime and memory always return 0.0 when running evaluation"; llm_sandbox/docker.py parses a three-field line and ON ANY PARSE FAILURE SILENTLY SETS ALL THREE TO 0.0, with backend_utils.py:539-543 also defaulting to 0.0. This is our own fail_open_defaults_conceal_their_own_corruption class sitting on the exact metric we would adopt, and it mandates an acceptance gate that runs the canonical human solutions first and asserts non-zero readings. (b) openjdk:21-jdk-bookworm NO LONGER EXISTS (Docker Hub API and registry manifest both 404, the official image having been deprecated) and BaseExecutionManager.setup() iterates ALL SIX languages unconditionally, so startup fails unless you --skip-setup or substitute eclipse-temurin:21-jdk. (c) generate_solution.py has `from time import time` at line 5 but calls time.sleep(...) at line 123, an AttributeError on the first submitted task. (8) REPO IS DEAD: 10 commits total, last 2025-10-24, no tags, no releases, 15 stars, four open issues with ZERO maintainer replies ever. A maintained fork exists (RidiculousBuffal/effibench-enhanced, Apache-2.0, pushed 2026-07-29) and may already carry fixes for all three blockers. (9) REVISIT THE credibility_score 1 ARITHMETIC, which was computed as "no venue located": arXiv 2505.13004 is still v1 only with the comment "Under Review" after 15 months, but the repo description reads [NeurIPS'25] and the README bibtex claims NeurIPS 2025. Artifact-side acceptance claims against an arXiv comment that was never updated - not conclusive either way, but the score should be re-derived. (10) AUTHOR-LINKAGE: Mingzhe Du co-authors both this entry and intake-951#record; they are NOT independent instruments and must not be counted as mutual corroboration. STAGE-2b ADDENDUM 2026-08-03 (dive 2b-D) - THE LINEAGE FINDING, and it changes how this cluster counts. EffiBench-X additionally inherits EffiBench (4 of 5 authors carry over). So Mercury -> EffiBench(-X) -> SWE-Perf IS ONE CONNECTED COMPONENT OF FOUR PAPERS, and Mercury is its ancestor and connector. CONSEQUENCE: the apparent field-wide consensus that "efficiency scores must be normalized against a reference distribution" is HALF ONE LINEAGE. The genuinely independent instruments DIVERGE sharply - ENAMEL (intake-970#record) normalizes against ONE EXPERT SOLUTION with censoring; EvalPerf (intake-971#record) against a CLUSTERED LADDER using instruction counts; GSO (intake-973#record) against a SINGLE HUMAN COMMIT in the same container; COFFE (intake-954#record) against THE BEST GROUND TRUTH, binary. COUNT THE DESIGN SPACE AS FOUR, NOT EIGHT. CLEAN NOTE: SWE-fficiency (intake-953#record, Harvard/GDM) is OUTSIDE the Du bloc and cited EvalPerf (UIUC), so the disputed normalization claim is a CROSS-LINEAGE MISREADING - a citation-hygiene failure, not a captured literature. THREE DISTINCT LIUS, DO NOT MERGE: Jiawei Liu (EvalPerf, UIUC), Qian Liu (Mercury/SWE-Perf, Sea AI Lab), Jinjian Liu (GSO, Berkeley). The "Liu et al., 2024" ambiguity is precisely how the original misattribution became plausible.

### Verdict

Adoptable and runnable: Apache-2.0 with a working self-host path, retargetable at a local endpoint via OPENAI_BASE_URL plus a dummy key, unpatched. Prefer it over intake-951#record where a licence-clean efficiency instrument is needed.
adopt_component. Unlike intake-951#record, the licence is confirmed Apache-2.0 and the harness ships a working self-host path, so it is genuinely adoptable rather than merely instructive. Not adopt_patterns alone, because we could actually run it. Not not_applicable or superseded - it complements the repo-scale benchmarks by covering algorithmic micro-efficiency across six languages.

## intake-953#record — SWE-fficiency: Can Language Models Optimize Real-World Repositories on Real Workloads?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06090
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Expanded from**: intake-939#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Most coding benchmarks emphasize WHAT to fix rather than HOW to fix code, so they measure the wrong thing for optimization work.
   - *unaffected*: Nothing in the correction disputes the WHAT-vs-HOW framing; the dive's targets are the citation trail, the aggregator, the harness licence, and the noise-filter mechanism.
   - anchor `matched span in https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06090`: "However, most benchmarks emphasize what to fix rather than how to fix code."
1. SWE-bench-strong agents do not transfer to efficiency work: GPT-5-mini with OpenHands achieved 0.019x of expert speedup while the same system scored 62.6% on SWE-bench Verified.
   - *overturned*: A STALE FIGURE SURVIVES INSIDE v3: prose in Sec 4.1 says GPT-5-mini 0.019x while v3 Tables 2 and 6 both say 0.039x. QUOTE v3 NUMBERS FROM TABLES ONLY. The non-transfer thrust survives at 0.039x.
2. Even when patches are functionally correct, the majority of edits are still slower than the expert - correctness gates hide this entirely.
   - *unaffected*: The correction leaves patch outcomes intact and cites them as evidence: its Table 3 patch-outcome row is byte-identical in both versions (19% / 5% / 44% / 33%).
3. Agents SATISFICE rather than exhaust budget: median trajectory action counts sit at 30-50 turns against a 100-action cap.
   - *unaffected*: No sentence of the correction mentions trajectories, action counts, or the 100-action cap; the satisficing claim is never disputed.
4. The expert-grounded speedup normalization acts as an effective noise filter in its own right.
   - *overturned*: The 'expert-normalized ratios are their own noise filter' claim is OVERTURNED and the mechanism misattributed: dividing by it is a per-instance rescaling that is variance-neutral by construction. The real mechanism is the paired within-container ratio.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-D. THE CENTRAL CORRECTION THIS ENTRY CARRIED IS ITSELF WRONG AND IS RETRACTED. (1) RETRACT THE ROW-CROSSING FINDING. This entry recorded that intake-939#record's citation of "Claude 4.5 Sonnet 81% correct but only 4.1% of expert speedup" had crossed rows, with 0.041x supposedly belonging to Gemini 2.5 Flash. Reading v1 directly: V1 TABLE 2 LISTS "CLAUDE 4.5 SONNET 0.041x" AND "GEMINI 2.5 FLASH 0.008x". The FAIR citation is a FAITHFUL v1 QUOTE ON BOTH LEGS. 0.041x migrating to Gemini 2.5 Flash in v3 is a coincidence, not the source of the citation. CAUSE OF THE ORIGINAL ERROR, recorded so it is not repeated: v1 names its models only inside the results table, so a search for "Sonnet" in v1 hits nothing but the bibliography. VERSION-PINNED QUOTES ARE NOT OPTIONAL IN THIS LITERATURE. (2) REWRITE THE VERSION CAVEAT. The v1-to-v3 shift is NOT a re-run; it is an AGGREGATION CHANGE plus a new top model. v3 adds "a per-instance lower bound of SR_min = 0.001 so that patches with near-zero speedup ratios do not dominate the harmonic mean", and Claude 4.5 Opus enters at 0.225x (so "less than 0.15x" becomes "less than 0.23x"). Evidence that it is not a re-run: Claude 4.5 Sonnet's SR moves 0.041x -> 0.116x, a factor of 2.8, WHILE ITS TABLE 3 PATCH-OUTCOME ROW IS BYTE-IDENTICAL IN BOTH VERSIONS (19% / 5% / 44% / 33%); Claude 4.1 Opus is 0.098x in both; and the task count is 498 in both. (3) THE AGGREGATOR IS THE REAL LESSON, and it is bigger than the axis question. A harmonic mean of ratios is dominated by its worst instance: with 497 instances at SR = 0.2, a single instance at 0.0005 pulls the mean to 0.1110 and at 0.001 to 0.1429, against 0.2000 if it too were 0.2. ONE PATHOLOGICAL INSTANCE HALVES THE SCORE. Recommendation: BAN harmonic-mean-of-ratios as an aggregator and require a bounded-contribution alternative (clipped arithmetic mean or trimmed mean). (4) THE "EXPERT-NORMALIZED RATIOS ARE THEIR OWN NOISE FILTER" CLAIM IS OVERTURNED, MECHANISM MISATTRIBUTED. run_evaluation.py:158-176 RE-MEASURES pre_edit_perf_runtime IN THE SAME CONTAINER as the post-edit run before computing the improvement at :275-276, so Speedup_LM is a PAIRED WITHIN-CONTAINER RATIO - that is where the cancellation happens. Speedup_gold is a STORED DATASET CONSTANT, so dividing by it is a per-instance rescaling that is VARIANCE-NEUTRAL BY CONSTRUCTION. Additionally, Appendix N contains NO UNNORMALIZED CONTROL ARM, so its design cannot test the claim at all. Our own bench-cpu.md already performs paired within-container ratios; the genuinely different mechanism worth pursuing is intake-939#record's affine drift correction against a stored reference pool, which addresses cross-epoch drift that paired ratios do not. (5) THE HARNESS IS LIVE AND Apache-2.0 - VERDICT adopt_patterns -> adopt_component, SCOPED. Stage 1 read it as "Coming Soon" because THE DEFAULT BRANCH IS swefficiency_base, NOT main. LICENSE byte-verified: "Copyright 2026 Google LLC / Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0", corroborated by pyproject.toml, PyPI swefficiency 1.0.0 and per-file headers; GitHub's NOASSERTION is a classifier artifact. ADOPT swefficiency/harness/cpu_assignment.py - 196 lines, standalone, and it produced correct NUMA-and-SMT-aware cpusets on our EPYC 9655 the first time it was executed (23 fully isolated 4-whole-core workers at threads_per_core=1). DECLINE the image corpus: GHCR manifests verify pandas 2.95 GB, matplotlib 2.46 GB, dask 2.40 GB, scipy 2.35 GB, averaging ~2.06 GB across 498 instances = ~1.03 TB compressed against 158 GB free. The DATASET carries no licence field. (6) THE PAPER CONTRADICTS ITS OWN CODE TWICE. Appendix F.2 states "no two vCPUs in the same group share a physical core", but run_validation.py:1130 calls allocate_whole_cores(..., threads_per_core=2), meaning each assigned core contributes BOTH SMT siblings - executing the module on our host, worker 0 receives cpuset_cpus='4,5,100,101'. The invariant the code actually holds is "no PHYSICAL CORE is shared ACROSS workers". And Appendix C's "deliberately no warm-up policy" is contradicted by run_validation.py:230 perf_warmup = True with a full discarded pass at :562 and :639. (7) v3 APPENDIX N REPORTS v1-REGIME NUMBERS: Table 11 gives Claude 3.7 Sonnet 0.0476-0.0480 and Gemini 2.5 Flash 0.00794, matching v1's 0.047x and 0.008x rather than v3's 0.024x and 0.041x, with no subset or alternative aggregation stated. THE PUBLISHED STABILITY EVIDENCE WAS NOT PRODUCED UNDER THE PUBLISHED AGGREGATION. (8) A STALE FIGURE SURVIVES INSIDE v3: the prose in Sec 4.1 says GPT-5-mini 0.019x while v3 Tables 2 and 6 both say 0.039x - the same sentence updated "0.15 to 0.23" but not the model number. QUOTE v3 NUMBERS FROM TABLES ONLY. (9) The "under 0.5% variance" claim holds as a coefficient of variation over n=3 but reaches 0.61% and 0.73% as max-minus-min relative spread for two models; and v3 Table 11's printed spread for Gemini is 10x its own max-minus-min. (10) ADD, and worth copying: anti-reward-hacking by AST-transforming workload scripts so each timing repetition runs in a FRESH CHILD PROCESS (Appendix L.2), defeating lru_cache and global-state leakage - the OPPOSITE doctrine to intake-951#record's deliberate warm-up, and the correct choice when ranking model output. ADD: the PR scrape cutoff is March 12 2025 and disjointness is from SWE-bench, NOT from training data - every expert patch is a merged public PR in numpy/pandas/scipy, inside every 2026 model's training window. ADD: workload annotation cost ~200 author-hours for 1041 instances, of which 498 survived validation. Mingzhe Du is the hub on three papers: Mercury (intake-972#record) and EffiBench-X share 3 authors (Du, Ng, Luu Anh Tuan); Mercury and SWE-Perf share 2 (Du, Qian Liu); SWE-Perf and EffiBench-X share 1 (Du). EffiBench-X additionally inherits EffiBench (4 of 5 authors carry over). So Mercury -> EffiBench(-X) -> SWE-Perf IS ONE CONNECTED COMPONENT OF FOUR PAPERS, and Mercury is its ancestor and connector. CONSEQUENCE: the apparent field-wide consensus that "efficiency scores must be normalized against a reference distribution" is HALF ONE LINEAGE. The genuinely independent instruments DIVERGE sharply - ENAMEL (intake-970#record) normalizes against ONE EXPERT SOLUTION with censoring; EvalPerf (intake-971#record) against a CLUSTERED LADDER using instruction counts; GSO (intake-973#record) against a SINGLE HUMAN COMMIT in the same container; COFFE (intake-954#record) against THE BEST GROUND TRUTH, binary. COUNT THE DESIGN SPACE AS FOUR, NOT EIGHT. CLEAN NOTE: SWE-fficiency (intake-953#record, Harvard/GDM) is OUTSIDE the Du bloc and cited EvalPerf (UIUC), so the disputed normalization claim is a CROSS-LINEAGE MISREADING - a citation-hygiene failure, not a captured literature. THREE DISTINCT LIUS, DO NOT MERGE: Jiawei Liu (EvalPerf, UIUC), Qian Liu (Mercury/SWE-Perf, Sea AI Lab), Jinjian Liu (GSO, Berkeley). The "Liu et al., 2024" ambiguity is precisely how the original misattribution became plausible. CLOSURE 2026-08-03 - THE APPENDIX-N ITEM IS RESOLVED, AND THE PREMISE WAS MISATTRIBUTED. This entry's Appendix N states that "conversion to normalized or percentage-based speedup metrics inherently reduces variability in final scores (Liu et al., 2024)". Its bibliography resolves that to arXiv 2408.06450 = EvalPerf (intake-971#record), NOT to ENAMEL. grep -ci "qiu|enamel|eff@k" over v3 returns 0, 0, 0 - IT NEVER CITES ENAMEL. AND THE ACTUAL CITATION ARGUES THE OPPOSITE. EvalPerf Sec A.2: relative speedup's applicability "becomes less clear across a broad range of tasks. This is because the degree of speedup varies significantly across different tasks ... This variability can lead to confusion." EvalPerf introduces DPS BECAUSE relative speedup is variable; its stability comes from HARDWARE PERFORMANCE COUNTERS and from SELECTING LARGE COMPUTATIONS, not from normalization. ENAMEL: NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE. Its single normalization sentence is scale-comparability only. Its variance reduction comes from R=6 repeats with the Hodges-Lehmann estimator and from Rao-Blackwellization of the bootstrap estimator - neither is normalization. THIRD-PARTY CORROBORATION OF OUR DERIVED MECHANISM, from GSO Appendix D (intake-973#record), a disjoint Berkeley group: "Our metric controls for machine-specific variation by comparing generated optimizations against expert developer implementations in the same environment, rather than measuring absolute speedups." => OUR DECLINE TO AMEND THE MEASUREMENT CONSTITUTION STANDS, on STRONGER grounds than the ones we declined on. This citation trail is recorded so the item cannot reopen.

### Verdict

Carry the harness PATTERNS only; the harness is not downloadable. IMPORTANT: this entry previously recorded a row-crossing defect in intake-939#record's citation — that correction was itself wrong and is RETRACTED. Do not re- propagate it.
adopt_patterns, not adopt_component, because the harness is NOT downloadable today - the GitHub repository is advertised as Coming Soon and the paper states release intent rather than completion, with no licence stated for the harness itself. The dataset is live on HuggingFace. The measurement doctrine transfers immediately regardless. Not not_applicable and not superseded - it is the repo-scale complement to intake-952#record algorithmic-scale instrument.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Correct the intake-939#record provenance note: the 4.1%-of-expert-speedup figure for Claude 4.5 Sonnet does not survive primary-source check against v3 (0.116x; 0.041x belongs to Gemini 2.5 Flash). The 81% correctness leg does survive. Record the version caveat rather than calling it a fabrication.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add an efficiency axis to the coder/architect ladder using expert-normalized speedup RATIOS rather than absolute latency - our current ladder cannot separate a model whose patches are correct and slower.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Evaluate the normalization argument as a MEASUREMENT-constitution amendment candidate: ratio-to-a-pinned-reference may be the principled answer to the co-residency ranking-instability finding, because a ratio cancels host-level drift that absolute tokens-per-second does not.

## intake-954#record — COFFE: A Code Efficiency Benchmark for Code Generation

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02827
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology)
- **Expanded from**: intake-939#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Wall-clock execution time is unusable as an efficiency measure because it is affected by process scheduling and disk IO and is not stable enough for solid comparison.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE, and the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO. The wall-clock objection stands on its own terms.
1. CPU instruction count has a more than 1000x smaller relative standard deviation than execution time (0.003-0.005% versus 2.37-5.65%) and is linearly correlated with execution time at Pearson 0.96-1.0.
   - *narrowed*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE, and the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO. THE APPLICABILITY LIMIT IS NO LONGER A CAVEAT — IT IS A MEASURED FALSIFICATION, RUN ON OUR OWN HARDWARE (in-session probe, gcc -O2, pinned with taskset). COFFE's Eq. 1 is an IDENTITY, not a model, and the inference it licenses — that 'the only factor related to the program is Instruction Count' — is false in general, because CPI is a property of the program-MACHINE PAIR, not of the program. The low variance of instruction count is real; what does not follow is that it therefore measures program efficiency on a given machine.
2. Instruction count does not increase even when program execution is slowed or stalled by external factors - i.e. it is co-residency-immune by construction.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE, and the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO. This statement is TRUE and is precisely why instruction count is not a proxy for time: a stalled program retires the same instructions. The claim is sound; the inference drawn from it in the entry is what the falsification removes.
3. Correctness benchmarks cannot discriminate efficiency; stressful tests must be synthesized to create duration spread.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE, and the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO.
4. Correctness and efficiency must be ONE fused metric, not two separate scores, otherwise high-correctness-low-efficiency and high-efficiency-low-correctness solutions are indistinguishable.
   - *unaffected*: 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE, and the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-C. verdict adopt_component STANDS but is NARROWED IN SCOPE; the entry's own pending test is discharged with a NO. (1) THE APPLICABILITY LIMIT IS NO LONGER A CAVEAT - IT IS A MEASURED FALSIFICATION, RUN ON OUR OWN HARDWARE. COFFE's Eq. 1 is an IDENTITY, not a model, and its inference that "the only factor related to the program is Instruction Count" is false in general because CPI is a property of the program-machine PAIR. In-session probe (gcc -O2, taskset -c 100, 8 repetitions each, 8192^2 f32 matrix sum): naive column-major = 444,837,196 instructions at IPC 0.24 and 483.5 ms; tiled 64x64 = 453,292,177 instructions (+1.90%) at IPC 0.84 and 171.1 ms - 2.83x FASTER WHILE EXECUTING MORE INSTRUCTIONS. IPC swings 3.5x between two implementations of the same computation on the same machine, and instruction count RANKS THE WRONG CANDIDATE FIRST, in exactly the memory-bound regime our kernels live in and on exactly the transformation (tiling) an autokernel loop generates. Separately, `perf stat -e instructions:u -- sleep 0.05` vs `sleep 0.5` shows a 10x wall difference for a -0.09% instruction difference - the structural analogue of GPU offload. INSTRUCTION COUNT IS PRECISE WITHOUT BEING ACCURATE. (2) THIS ENTRY'S recommended_action 4 ("test the constant-CPI assumption before porting") IS THEREFORE DISCHARGED WITH A NO. Rewrite it as a closed finding rather than a pending test. THREE-WAY APPLICABILITY VERDICT: (a) GPU KERNEL CANDIDATES - NO, structurally blind, because a HIP kernel retires ZERO host x86 instructions and Cirron's exclude_kernel plus inherit=0 additionally drop the ROCm runtime's kernel-mode work and every helper thread, so two candidates differing 10x in device time can be within noise; the GPU analogue (rocprof SQ_INSTS_*) suffers the SAME inversion and is corroboration only, never the ranking scalar. (b) CPU DECODE BENCHMARKING - NO, and actively misleading, because bandwidth-bound decode stalls on DRAM and instruction count is by construction insensitive to NUMA placement, interleave, mmap placement, thread topology, prefetch and quant repacking for locality - every recorded win we hold moves CPI, not instruction count, and several move instruction count the wrong way. (c) GENERATED-CODE EVAL LADDER - YES, and this is where the instrument belongs: short-running single-threaded cache-resident algorithmic Python genuinely is a near-constant-CPI regime, which is why Pearson reaches 0.96-1.0, and it makes the score immune to the co-residency problem that currently prevents our ladder carrying a runtime axis at all. NARROW THE VERDICT TO (c). (3) A SALVAGEABLE USE ON THE PATHS WHERE RANKING FAILS: instruction count as an A/A CONTAMINATION DETECTOR. The same binary under the same recipe should retire the same instructions; a material delta means the two runs did DIFFERENT WORK (different token counts, a fallback, a different code path) and the wall-clock comparison is void. A cheap integrity gate we do not currently have. (4) THE UNRESOLVED ENVIRONMENT QUESTION IS CLOSED - YES. perf_event_paranoid is 1, /usr/bin/perf is present, container CapEff is 0x0; cirronlib.cpp was compiled and its exact perf_event_open path executed, giving 204,524,295 / 204,520,723 / 204,520,245 instructions across three trials (RSD 0.001%) with NO sudo and NO --privileged. Note it does NOT work for ROCm workloads, and that is a SEMANTICS problem, not a permissions one. Inside Docker it additionally needs CAP_PERFMON or seccomp relaxation. (5) MEASUREMENT SEMANTICS OF THE INSTRUMENT, absent from the paper and load-bearing for anyone adopting it: exclude_kernel=1 and exclude_hv=1 mean USER-SPACE ONLY; perf_event_open with pid=0 means CALLING THREAD ONLY; attr.inherit is never set so CHILD PROCESSES AND OTHER THREADS ARE NOT COUNTED; and TOTAL_TIME_RUNNING is read and DISCARDED, so there is NO MULTIPLEXING-SCALING CHECK and counts silently under-report if the PMU multiplexes. Failure mode is a hard raise rather than a silent zero, which is good. COFFE forks a fresh process per measurement, but at function level exec() sits OUTSIDE the collector while at file level the whole exec sits INSIDE - an ASYMMETRIC SCOPE BETWEEN LEVELS that must be normalised if levels are mixed. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE LADDER: a numpy or torch solution dispatching to multithreaded OpenBLAS is scored on the main thread only and looks ARTIFICIALLY CHEAP. (6) THE PROVENANCE FLAG IS PROMOTED FROM SUSPICION TO VERIFIED. COFFE Sec 1 Challenge 2 states verbatim: "Shypula et al. [75] find that two single time measurements of the code solution on the same environment can differ as much as 1.91x", where [75] is intake-950#record. ONE SOURCE, NOT TWO - and the restatement is LOSSY, reframing a 95th-percentile tail (mean 1.12, sd 0.36 over 500 pairs) as a bound. Anyone citing COFFE for 1.91x inherits the degraded version. COFFE's OWN relative-standard-deviation table IS original and independent, and it REPLICATES CONSERVATIVELY on our host: instruction-count RSD 0.000007%/0.000035% against wall-clock RSD 1.16%/1.67%, a stability ratio of 4.8e4 to 1.7e5 versus the claimed 1e3. (7) THE "5-SECOND PER-MEASUREMENT WALL CAP" IN reported_results IS MIS-SCOPED: the 5 s cap is Sec 3.4, for BENCHMARK CONSTRUCTION. The released EVALUATION path uses max(0.1, 10.0 x 5) = 50 s. (8) Apache-2.0 is now BYTE-VERIFIED from Coffe/LICENSE:1-3 rather than read from the README. The 12-run / drop-max-and-min / RSD-over-10 protocol is confirmed to match the code for BOTH metrics (a 100-run untrimmed variant exists but is not imported), so the RSD comparison is genuinely apples-to-apples. The anti-circularity control is confirmed. (9) DESIGN CONFLICT WITH intake-951#record, unresolved and carried to planning: COFFE argues correctness and efficiency must be FUSED into one efficient@k capped at pass@k. DIVE-D established that all four instruments in this cluster ALREADY gate at the instance level, so the framed conflict is largely false - but COFFE's efficient@k is additionally BINARY (faster than the best ground-truth solution, or not), which DISCARDS MAGNITUDE: a 1.01x win and a 100x win score identically, so the metric cannot reward the large algorithmic wins that are the point.

### Verdict

Adopt as a runnable instruction-count rig, but NARROWED: its Eq. 1 is an identity, not a model, and its inference that instruction count is the only program-related factor is falsified — measured on our own hardware. Do not use instruction count as a stand-in for wall-clock.
adopt_component. It is an Apache-2.0 working instruction-count measurement rig we could point at our own evaluation tasks, which makes it more than a pattern donor. The efficient@k construction additionally answers the design question of whether to bolt efficiency on as a second score - it argues no - though intake-951#record argues the opposite and the disagreement is unresolved. Task provenance is competitive-programming and HumanEval, so the ladder value is the METRIC and the harness, not the task set.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/architect-model-selection-bench.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add the efficiency axis to the coder/architect ladder as efficient@k over INSTRUCTION COUNTS via Cirron/perf, NOT wall-clock - this makes the metric immune to our co-residency and NUMA contention problem and to the ranking swings recorded at intake-939#record and intake-950#record.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Adopt the trimmed-repetition protocol (12 runs, drop minimum and maximum, mean of the remaining 10) and the ratio-only scoring rule, so scores stay comparable across our heterogeneous hosts. Absolute counts are explicitly rejected by the paper as machine-dependent.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Evaluate STGen separately as a way to add duration-spread tests to our EXISTING correctness suites - it is the piece that fixes small tests producing spurious speedups, and it is Apache-2.0.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] BEFORE porting instruction counting to llama-bench, test the constant-CPI assumption against our bandwidth-bound CPU decode finding - the metric may be valid for generated code and invalid for our inference kernels.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-08-03 DIVE-A. Architecture facts CONFIRMED from config.json; two additions and one scope correction. (1) The architecture ground truth is confirmed field by field against the raw config.json: no rope_theta, no rope_scaling and no partial_rotary_factor of any kind, with d_rel 16, rel_extent 1024, log_scaling_n_floor 128000 and log_scaling_alpha 0.1 instead; use_sconv true with sconv_kernel_size 4; mtp_config with num_nextn_predict_layers 8; 42 layers; 256 experts top-6 plus 2 shared; hidden 4096; model_max_length 1048576. (2) THE MTP FINDING NEEDS A SCOPE CORRECTION THAT MAKES IT MOOT FOR llama.cpp. The 8-layer MTP block and the separate mtp.safetensors are real, so self-speculation is architecturally available - BUT the llama.cpp conversion path in PR #25731 EXPLICITLY STRIPS THEM: conversion/inkling.py declares _SKIP_PREFIXES including "model.mtp." and filter_tensors() returns None for matches, so the MTP weights never reach the GGUF. Record the capability as present in the base model and UNAVAILABLE through the GGUF path. (3) THE ACCEPTABLE-USE POLICY WAS FETCHED (this entry listed it as unresolved) AND IS NOT A BLOCKER. thinkingmachines.ai/model-acceptable-use-policy binds independently of Apache-2.0 - "By accessing, downloading, or using any Model Materials, you agree to be bound by this Model AUP" - and is a separate document, never mentioning Apache-2.0 and not incorporated by reference. Its prohibitions are conduct-based (illegal activity, child safety, weapons and violent extremism, automated consequential decisions about individuals, surveillance and deanonymisation, fraud and impersonation, unlicensed professional practice) with NO restriction on commercial use, self-hosting, redistribution or fine-tuning, and NO enforcement or termination clause. Record the licence as "Apache-2.0 plus a binding behavioural AUP". (4) CONTEXT LENGTH IS DISPUTED, not settled: config.json says 1048576 but Artificial Analysis reports 256K for Inkling-Small (1M for the flagship). Do not file 1M as established. (5) THE VENDOR BENCHMARK TABLE NEEDS SPLITTING: 80.2% SWE-bench Verified is INKLING-SMALL and 77.6% is the 975B FLAGSHIP - Small BEATS the flagship, and the flagship is also lower on HLE, Terminal Bench and GDPval-AA. Attach the methodology caveat that all evals ran at effort 0.99 and temperature 1.0 with a 256K max-token trajectory limit, no scaffold disclosed. (6) ADD the one favourable architectural fact for long-context roles: sliding_window_size 512 with local_layer_ids covering 35 of 42 layers means only SEVEN global attention layers, so KV-cache cost is far lower than a 276B model implies. (7) ADD genuinely third-party evidence, distinguishing it from the vendor pages which merely restate Thinking Machines' own figures: Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index 40 for Inkling-Small against 41 for the flagship, DeepSeek V4 Flash 40, MiniMax-M3 44 at 23B active, GLM-5.2 51 at 40B active; AA-Briefcase 917 Elo against the flagship's 839; roughly 24K output tokens per task, notably terse against DeepSeek V4 Flash's ~45K. (8) ROLE-CANDIDATE CONTEXT (operator's framing): a v9 port would cost THREE new subsystems, not one - a content-dependent relative attention bias replacing RoPE, a per-layer short-convolution state cache alongside KV, and shared-expert-sink MoE routing - and the upstream PR carrying them is rejected in its current form. See intake-941#record and intake-942#record for the port-cost and role verdicts.

### Verdict

worth_investigating, consistent with intake-941#record and intake-942#record. The port cost is the gate and this entry raises the estimate. The one strongly favourable finding is the shipped MTP block, which makes self-speculation available if a port ever happens - pairing with the draft-compat workflow rather than requiring an external drafter. Not not_applicable: Apache-2.0, a CPU-favourable 12B-active shape, and a capability (1M context plus native audio and image) we have no incumbent for.
